   - Node **L** connects to node **M**.
   - Node **M** connects to node **I**.

   - **E → P → J**
   - **E → P → I → J**
   - **E → P → B → C → H → I → J**
   - **E → P → B → C → N → Y → O → I → J**
   - **E → P → B → C → N → O → I → J**

   - Removing **P** will break three main paths.
   - Removing **I** will break all paths that pass through **P → I → J**.
   - Removing **B** will break paths that go through **P → B → C → H → I → J** and **P → B → C → N → O → I → J**.
   - To ensure all paths are broken, we need to strategically remove nodes.

4. **Determine the Minimum Cut Set:**

     - **E → P → J**.
     - **E → P → I → J**.
     - **E → P → B → C → H → I → J**.
     - **E → P → B → C → N → Y → O → I → J**.
     - **E → P → B → C → N → O → I → J**.

   There are no other minimal combinations that disconnect **E** and **J** without removing these nodes.

     1. L → E → P → J
     2. L → E → P → H → J
     3. L → E → P → C → J (assuming C → J through some other intermediary)
     4. L → E → M → J

     - (E, P): This removes the direct path L → E → P → J and the indirect paths through H.

  - Path 1: L → E → P → J is cut at (E, P).
  - Path 2: L → E → P → H → J is cut at (E, P).
  - Path 3: L → E → P → C → J does not exist without the edge (E, P).
  - Path 4: L → E → M → J is cut at (E, M).

     1. H -> G -> F -> M -> L -> E -> B -> N
     2. H -> B -> N
     3. H -> G -> F -> X -> K -> J -> I -> C -> O -> N
     4. H -> G -> F -> X -> K -> J -> D -> P -> C -> O -> N

     - F: Appears in paths 1, 3, and 4.
     - B: Appears in paths 1 and 2.

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (B and H), I will follow these steps:

1. Identify all paths between the two black nodes B and H.
2. Ensure that removing the selected edges breaks all existing paths between B and H.
3. Confirm that no smaller set of edges can achieve the same disconnection.

### Step-by-step reasoning:

1. **Identify all paths between B and H:**

   - Path 1: B → N → C → I → P → J → D → M → E → F → K → G → H
   - Path 2: B → N → Y → L → E → F → K → G → H
   - Path 3: B → N → C → I → P → J → M → E → F → K → G → H
   - Path 4: B → N → C → O → P → J → M → E → F → K → G → H

2. **Determine the critical edges:**

   To disconnect B and H, we need to remove edges such that all paths between them are broken. We can achieve this by removing edges that are common in all paths or that create bottlenecks.

   - Removing (B, N) will break Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Removing (F, K) will break Paths 1, 2, and 3.
   - Removing (K, G) will break Paths 1, 2, and 3.

   By removing (B, N) and (K, G), we ensure that all paths between B and H are broken.

3. **Verify the minimal cut set:**

   - Removing (B, N) and (K, G) ensures that there are no remaining paths between B and H.
   - No smaller set of edges can achieve the same disconnection because removing only one of these edges would leave alternative paths intact.

### Final Answer:
Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(B, N), (K, G)]

### Graph Analysis

1. **Paths between \( N \) and \( P \)**:
   - **Path 1**: \( N \to B \to E \to P \)
   - **Path 2**: \( N \to B \to I \to P \)
   - **Path 3**: \( N \to C \to I \to P \)
   - **Path 4**: \( N \to C \to H \to I \to P \)
   - **Path 5**: \( N \to C \to H \to G \to F \to K \to X \to J \to P \)
   - **Path 6**: \( N \to C \to H \to G \to F \to E \to P \)
   - **Path 7**: \( N \to C \to H \to I \to J \to D \to P \)
   - **Path 8**: \( N \to C \to H \to G \to J \to P \)
   - **Path 9**: \( N \to O \to E \to P \)
   - **Path 10**: \( N \to O \to E \to J \to P \)

### Path Grouping

From the above paths, we can group them into disjoint sets based on shared nodes:

1. **Set 1**: Connects through \( E \) (Paths 1, 6, 9, 10).
2. **Set 2**: Connects through \( I \) (Paths 2, 3, 4, 7).
3. **Set 3**: Connects through \( H \) and \( J \) (Paths 5, 8).
4. **Set 4**: Connects directly through \( B \) and \( I \) (Path 2 is also part of Set 2, but we need to consider any unique route).

### Determining the Minimum Cut Set

To disconnect \( N \) and \( P \), we need to sever all paths between them:
- Removing \( E \) will block all paths in Set 1.
- Removing \( I \) will block all paths in Set 2.
- Removing either \( H \) or \( J \) will block all paths in Set 3 since they are interconnected.

Since \( H \) and \( J \) are interconnected via \( F, G, K, X \), we can prioritize removing one of these to ensure minimal disruption. By removing \( H \), we block multiple paths involving \( H \) and \( J \) and thus requires removal of one more necessary node.

Therefore, the minimal cut set will include removing:
1. \( E \) (to block Set 1)
2. \( I \) (to block Set 2)
3. and either \( H \) or \( J \) (to block Set 3).

### Verification of Disconnection

After removing \( E \), \( I \), and \( H \):
- All paths through \( E \) are blocked.
- All paths through \( I \) are blocked.
- All paths through \( H \) and \( J \) are blocked.

Thus, no remaining paths exist between \( N \) and \( P \).

### Final Answer

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [E, I, H].

2. **Paths via Intermediate Nodes:**
   - H → G → E → B → N
   - H → G → E → L → M → J → P → I → O → C → B → N
   - H → G → E → L → F → K → J → P → I → O → C → B → N
   - H → I → P → J → K → F → X → E → B → N
   - H → I → P → J → K → F → E → B → N

- **Edge (B, N):**
  - Removing this will eliminate the direct path from B to N.

- **Edge (G, H):**

- **Edge (I, H):**

2. **Paths H → G → E → L → M → J → P → I → O → C → B → N** and **H → G → E → L → F → K → J → P → I → O → C → B → N** are broken by removing (G, H).

4. **Path H → I → P → J → K → F → E → B → N** is broken by removing (I, H).

### Verification:
- Removing only two out of the three edges will still leave an alternative path from H to N. For instance, if we do not remove the edge (I, H), the path H → I → P → J → K → F → X → E → B → N still remains. Similarly, if we do not remove (G, H), the path H → G → E → B → N still remains.

Thus, **all three edges must be removed to ensure there is no path left between H and N**.

### Final Answer:
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(G, H), (I, H), (B, N)].

     - E -> G -> F -> K -> X -> B -> C -> O -> P -> I
     - E -> M -> J -> D -> P -> I

     - Any path from E to I must pass through one or more of the nodes G, H, B, J, M, or J.
     - Removing G or H disconnects the direct path E -> H -> I.
     - Removing M or J disconnects multiple paths that route through these nodes.
     - Removing B disconnects the path E -> G -> F -> K -> X -> B -> C -> O -> P -> I.
     - Removing P disconnects the paths that route through P.

   - Removing G and P ensures that all paths between E and I are broken.
     - Removing G blocks: E -> G -> F -> K -> X -> B -> C -> O -> P -> I.
     - Removing P blocks: E -> M -> J -> P -> I, and other paths that pass through P.

   - Any smaller set, such as removing only one of G or P, would leave at least one path between E and I intact.
   - Therefore, the minimum cut set requires removing both G and P.

   - Verify no shorter path exists:
     - Checking through other nodes (E, P, B, C) does not yield a shorter path.

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify the Target Nodes:**
   - The two target nodes are I (black) and E (black).

2. **Trace All Paths Between I and E:**
   - Path 1: I -> H -> G -> X -> K -> F -> B -> N -> Y -> C -> P -> O -> B -> E
   - Path 2: I -> H -> G -> X -> K -> F -> E
   - Path 3: I -> H -> C -> Y -> N -> B -> E
   - Path 4: I -> H -> C -> P -> O -> B -> E

3. **Group Paths Based on Common Nodes:**
   - Path 1 and Path 2 share the nodes I -> H -> G -> X -> K -> F.
   - Path 3 and Path 4 share the nodes I -> H -> C.

4. **Identify Critical Nodes:**
   - To break all paths, we need to remove at least one node from each group of paths.
   - Removing node H will break Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Removing node F will break Paths 1 and 2.
   - Removing node C will break Paths 3 and 4.

5. **Determine the Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing H alone is sufficient to break all paths between I and E.

### Final Answer:

Image name: SEIS_task2_gt2_PAIR2
Task: task2_min_nodes_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 17
--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed in order to completely disconnect the two black nodes (G and N), we analyze the paths between them and identify the essential nodes that, when removed, will break all paths.

### Path Analysis
1. From G to N via H and C:
   - G -> H -> C -> N
2. From G to N via H, I, and O:
   - G -> H -> I -> O -> N
3. From G to N via E and B:
   - G -> E -> B -> N
4. From G to N via E, F, and M:
   - G -> E -> F -> M -> N
5. From G to N via P, I, and O:
   - G -> P -> I -> O -> N
6. From G to N via P, J, and I:
   - G -> P -> J -> I -> O -> N

### Essential Nodes for Disconnection
To disconnect G and N, we must sever all the above paths. None of these paths overlap in a way that allows us to block all paths by removing just one node. For instance:
- Removing H blocks the first and second paths but not the others.
- Removing E blocks the third and fourth paths but not the others.

We observe that removing nodes H and E will collectively block all paths between G and N.

### Verification
Removing nodes H and E:
- Path 1 is blocked (G -> H -> C -> N)
- Path 2 is blocked (G -> H -> I -> O -> N)
- Path 3 is blocked (G -> E -> B -> N)
- Path 4 is blocked (G -> E -> F -> M -> N)
- Path 5 is still open but includes I and O hence they are already blocked by Path 2
- Path 6 is still open but includes P, J, and I hence they are already blocked by Path 2

Thus, removing nodes H and E effectively breaks all paths between G and N.

### Conclusion
The minimum cut set required to disconnect the two black nodes (G and N) has been identified.

**Final Answer: Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [H, E]**
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': ['E', 'H']}
